```json
{
  "parentUuid": "59173856-e5af-413b-91d1-27dbbf8cff84",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520/projects/ec_hte_llm_annotation/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520/projects/ec_hte_llm_annotation/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nWe prove that correcting LLM annotation error with a single pooled confusion matrix---standard practice when LLM labels serve as effect modifiers in heterogeneous treatment effect (HTE) analysis---introduces subgroup-level bias exactly proportional to the product of misclassification heterogeneity and CATE heterogeneity ($\\Delta\\pi \\times \\Delta\\tau$), an exact population-level identity verified at $R^2 = 0.994$ ($K{=}2$).\nIn 50\\% of experimental configurations, global correction worsens subgroup CATE bias, with amplification up to 2.86$\\times$ ($K{=}2$, difference-in-means) and 5.9$\\times$ ($K{=}4$, CausalForest).\nA natural solution enabled by this insight, Error-Corrected Heterogeneous Treatment Effects (EC-HTE), combines hierarchical Bayesian subgroup-stratified confusion matrix estimation with CausalForestDML, reducing $|\\text{bias}|$ from 0.059 to 0.010 while maintaining nominal coverage ($\\geq$0.91 across all configurations) under extreme heterogeneity ($K{=}2$); a computable diagnostic (AUC $= 0.86$) identifies when global correction increases bias from per-subgroup misclassification rates alone, actionable without adopting EC-HTE.\nComposed with Prediction-Powered Conditional Inference (PPCI) for dual covariate/outcome error, the two corrections are each necessary and jointly sufficient, reducing $|\\text{bias}|$ from 0.179 to 0.007.\nThe correction is estimator-agnostic and robust to non-additive treatment effects: replacing CausalForest with the R-learner yields comparable bias reduction (89\\%), and a multiplicative DGP yields nominal coverage (0.955).\nEC-HTE's advantage is conditional on sufficient error heterogeneity; when $\\Delta\\pi$ is small, global correction suffices.\nWe validate across 520 synthetic configurations (100 Monte Carlo runs each), a semi-synthetic TweetEval experiment ($N{=}5{,}000$), and four open-source LLMs (7B--9B) confirming $\\Delta\\pi$ heterogeneity on real annotations.\n\\end{abstract}",
        "numLines": 9,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 9
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/abstract.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",
  "uuid": "fa195e7b-6034-464a-9d85-e9f4253dd6c8",
```

```json
  "attachment": {
    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
```

[Director 指令] [Patch] Civil Comments 第二数据集 Δπ 结果写入论文（等 Reviewer 确认后 finalize）

**目标：强化 C1 回应——"两个 NLP 数据集验证子群异质误分类"**

**不需要调整整体叙事。** Reviewer 已确认论文 framing 正确——Table 1 主结果来自 synthetic DGP（EC-HTE 在高异质+平衡子群下确实 RMSE 最优），TweetEval/Civil Comments 是补充验证。

**写入内容：**

1. **§5.5 Multi-LLM Validation（主文，加 1-2 句）**
   当前 §5.5 报告 4 个开源 LLM 在 TweetEval 上的 Δπ。加一句说明第二数据集验证：
   "We replicate this finding on Civil Comments (toxicity detection): three proprietary LLMs (GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, GPT-3.5-turbo) exhibit Δπ ranging from 0.113 to 0.160 across text-feature subgroups, confirming that subgroup-heterogeneous misclassification is a structural property of LLM annotation across datasets and model families."
   
   这样 §5.5 覆盖：4 开源 LLM × TweetEval + 3 闭源 LLM × Civil Comments = 7 个 LLM × 2 个数据集。

2. **Appendix — 新 section "Civil Comments Validation"**
   - 数据集描述（Civil Comments, 3000 samples, toxicity detection）
   - 子群定义（text_length × has_question, K=4）
   - 混淆矩阵异质性表（3 LLM × Δ_FPR / Δ_FNR / 总 Δπ）
   - 简短讨论：与 TweetEval 模式一致

3. **Abstract — 可选微调**
   如果空间允许，"across X LLMs" 的数字从之前的数量更新为包含 Civil Comments 的总数。

**不写入的：**
- RMSE 对比结果（与 TweetEval 模式一致，不提供新 insight）
- Coverage 对比（高误分类下的结果需要 Reviewer 确认后再决定）
- 不改变 EC-HTE 的 RMSE 叙事

**约束：** 主文 ≤ 8 页，编译验证，引用需要验证。等 Reviewer 确认子群定义和 Δπ 计算后再 commit。

```text
99	
100	\subsection{Open-Source Multi-LLM Validation}
101	\label{sec:multilm_opensource}
102	
103	To verify that misclassification heterogeneity and EC-HTE's correction generalize beyond proprietary APIs, we annotate 500 TweetEval sentiment tweets with four open-source LLMs: Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3, and Gemma-2-9B-IT.
104	Each model independently classifies tweets using a 3-class prompt (positive/neutral/negative); we define a balanced binary label $Z{=}1$ if the true label $\geq 1$ (neutral or positive vs.\ negative), yielding $P(Z{=}1) \approx 0.65$.
105	Per-subgroup confusion matrices are estimated directly from each model's annotations against gold labels.
106	The EC-HTE pipeline---HB confusion matrix estimation followed by CausalForestDML---is identical to the synthetic experiments in Table~\ref{tab:main_results}.
107	All four models exhibit subgroup-heterogeneous misclassification ($\Delta\pi \in [0.125, 0.165]$, $K{=}4$), with consistent subgroup error rankings across architectures (Table~\ref{tab:multilm_cm}).
108	Correction improves 95\% CI coverage from 0.714 (naive average) to 0.939 ($+$22.5 percentage points, $K{=}2$, $n_{\text{expert}}{=}1{,}000$), restoring near-nominal inference across all four models.
109	Under the moderate $\Delta\pi$ observed in these real LLM annotations, global correction suffices for RMSE.
110	Stratified correction achieves lower RMSE than the naive estimator only at $n_{\text{expert}} \geq 1{,}000$ ($K{=}2$); global correction achieves comparable or lower RMSE than HB EC-HTE at all budgets under this moderate heterogeneity, consistent with the budget guidelines in Appendix~\ref{sec:budget}.
111	Full per-model results appear in Appendix~\ref{app:multilm_opensource}.
```

```text
740	\section{Comparison with PPI Correction}
741	\label{app:ppi_comparison}
742	
743	PPI~\cite{angelopoulos2023ppi} constructs confidence intervals by combining a large set of ML predictions with a smaller set of gold-standard labels, using the gold labels to debias the ML-based estimator.
744	Applied to our setting, PPI treats the LLM-annotated effect modifier as the ML prediction and the expert labels as the gold standard.
745	Two variants arise naturally: \emph{PPI-global} pools all expert labels to estimate a single rectifier (analogous to our Global baseline), and \emph{PPI-subgroup} estimates per-subgroup rectifiers (analogous to Stratified MLE).
746	
747	Under the confusion-matrix parameterization we adopt, DSL's measurement error correction reduces to confusion matrix inversion.
748	Pooled DSL applies a single estimated confusion matrix to all subgroups, producing the same corrected label distribution as our Global baseline; per-subgroup DSL estimates a separate confusion matrix for each subgroup and inverts it independently, equivalent to our Stratified MLE.
749	EC-HTE's contribution over both PPI-subgroup and per-subgroup DSL is hierarchical partial pooling: rather than estimating each subgroup's confusion matrix in isolation, EC-HTE places a shared prior across subgroups, borrowing strength to reduce variance while preserving subgroup-specific bias correction.
750	This mechanism is outside both the PPI and DSL frameworks.
751	
752	The key difference between PPI and confusion-matrix inversion is that PPI does not invert the confusion matrix; instead, it uses the gold labels to directly debias the estimand.
753	This avoids the matrix inversion step that can amplify noise at small $n_{\text{expert}}$, but it also means PPI cannot exploit the structure of the misclassification process to reduce bias.
754	PPI-global inherits the same $\Delta\pi \times \Delta\tau$ bias amplification as Global correction (Theorem~\ref{thm:bias_identity}), confirming that the ``debiasing hurts'' mechanism operates regardless of the inference framework.
755	
```

```json
  "toolUseResult": "{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"experiments\\\": {\\\"exp-023\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T23:27:31.475Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T23:29:42.293Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Under heterogeneous-τ DGP (Δτ varies 0.1–0.6 across subgroups), EC-HTE's subgroup-stratified CM correction outperforms Global pooled correction in bias because Global's single correction factor over-corrects low-Δτ subgroups and under-corrects high-Δτ subgroups; EC-HTE should also win RMSE in high-Δτ subgroups where bias dominates variance\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-008\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"dgp\\\": \\\"heterogeneous-tau\\\", \\\"N\\\": 5000, \\\"P_Z1\\\": 0.652, \\\"K\\\": 4, \\\"subgroups\\\": [\\\"False_medium\\\", \\\"False_short\\\", \\\"True_medium\\\", \\\"True_short\\\"], \\\"delta_tau\\\": {\\\"False_medium\\\": 0.1, \\\"False_short\\\": 0.1, \\\"True_medium\\\": 0.3, \\\"True_short\\\": 0.6}, \\\"n_expert_sweep\\\": [200, 500, 1000], \\\"mc_seeds\\\": 50, \\\"hb_method\\\": \\\"MAP-EB Gamma(3,1)\\\", \\\"bias_echte_wins\\\": \\\"3/3 budgets\\\", \\\"bias_reduction_n500\\\": \\\"68% vs Global (0.0099 vs 0.0314)\\\", \\\"bias_reduction_n200\\\": \\\"32% vs Global (0.0248 vs 0.0365)\\\", \\\"bias_reduction_n1000\\\": \\\"55% vs Global (0.0130 vs 0.0286)\\\", \\\"rmse_echte_wins\\\": \\\"0/3 aggregate\\\", \\\"rmse_true_short_n1000\\\": {\\\"global\\\": 0.1693, \\\"echte\\\": 0.1637, \\\"improvement\\\": \\\"-3.3%\\\"}, \\\"rmse_true_short_z0_n1000\\\": {\\\"global\\\": 0.2577, \\\"echte\\\": 0.2117, \\\"improvement\\\": \\\"-17.8%\\\"}, \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"EC-HTE wins bias at all budgets. High-Δτ subgroup (True_short) RMSE wins at n≥500. Aggregate RMSE loss driven by low-Δτ subgroups where stratified variance cost exceeds small bias gain.\\\"}}, \\\"exp-022\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T19:43:54.613Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T19:44:39.795Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"EC-HTE's hierarchical Bayes confusion matrix estimation is insensitive to Gamma hyperprior specification: varying shape∈{1,2,3,5,10} and scale∈{0.5,1,2} produces <10% RMSE variation, confirming the method is not driven by prior choice\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-001\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"rmse_range_reasonable\\\": \\\"6.0%\\\", \\\"rmse_range_all\\\": \\\"17.3%\\\", \\\"gamma31_rmse\\\": 0.0841, \\\"gamma31_rank\\\": \\\"8/15\\\", \\\"coverage_range\\\": \\\"0.925-0.938\\\", \\\"hb_beats_mle\\\": \\\"9/15\\\", \\\"best_config\\\": \\\"Gamma(1,0.5)\\\", \\\"best_rmse\\\": 0.0825}}, \\\"exp-021\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T17:50:05.855Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T17:50:34.986Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Table 1 RMSE results are stable under doubling of MC seeds (50→100); median cell change <5% confirms 50-seed results are not artifacts of sampling variance\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-001\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"total_cells_compared\\\": 94, \\\"median_delta_pct\\\": 3.2, \\\"max_delta_pct\\\": 25.5, \\\"cells_under_5pct\\\": \\\"79/94 (84%)\\\", \\\"cells_under_10pct\\\": \\\"93/94 (99%)\\\", \\\"outlier\\\": \\\"K=4/extreme/n=100/HB base (-25.5%, known numerically unstable)\\\", \\\"method_note\\\": \\\"EB instead of Gibbs (<0.002 CM difference, 10s vs 114min)\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"100-seed stability test for Table 1. 84% cells <5% change, 99% <10%. Used EB estimator for speed (10s vs 114min Gibbs); EB-Gibbs CM difference <0.002. Non-HB methods identical. Addresses RD2-M4.\\\"}, \\\"exp-020\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T16:14:37.738Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T16:15:14.257Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"κ=100 condition number guard provides sufficient safety margin for EC-HTE; empirical sweep will show guard rarely triggers under moderate misclassification and the exact threshold is not critical for well-conditioned problems\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-001\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"max_cond_number\\\": 1.89, \\\"mean_cond_number\\\": 1.446, \\\"p99_cond_number\\\": 1.893, \\\"guard_trigger_rate_all_kappa\\\": 0, \\\"rmse_hb\\\": 0.0759, \\\"rmse_global\\\": 0.0765, \\\"rmse_ratio\\\": 1.048, \\\"numerical_instability_rate\\\": 0, \\\"safety_margin_at_kappa100\\\": \\\"53x\\\", \\\"stress_test_near_random_max_cond\\\": 1125.3, \\\"stress_test_near_random_triggers_kappa100\\\": true}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"κ sweep across {10,25,50,100,200,500,1000}: guard never triggers in moderate regime (max cond=1.89, 53× below κ=100). All κ values produce identical RMSE (HB=0.076, Global=0.077). Zero numerical instability. Stress test: guard only activates with near-random labeling (π_s≈0.4-0.5, n_expert=50, max cond=1125). κ=100 validated as safe default. Adaptive formula κ=10·√(n_expert/K) recovers κ=100 for this regime. Addresses RD2-W4.\\\"}, \\\"exp-019\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T15:38:56.994Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T15:39:34.345Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Theorem 1 bias decomposition R² remains high (>0.95) across P(Z=1) range [0.1, 0.5], and EC-HTE RMSE advantage over global persists but degrades at low P(Z=1) due to insufficient minority-class expert labels\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-001\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"theorem1_r2\\\": {\\\"K2\\\": {\\\"pz0.1\\\": 0.998, \\\"pz0.2\\\": 0.989, \\\"pz0.3\\\": 0.994, \\\"pz0.4\\\": 0.995, \\\"pz0.5\\\": 0.995}, \\\"K4\\\": {\\\"pz0.1\\\": 0.984, \\\"pz0.2\\\": 0.976, \\\"pz0.3\\\": 0.985, \\\"pz0.4\\\": 0.984, \\\"pz0.5\\\": 0.992}}, \\\"theorem1_r2_range\\\": \\\"0.976-0.998 (all >0.95)\\\", \\\"rmse_ratio_hb_over_global\\\": {\\\"K2\\\": {\\\"pz0.1\\\": 0.92, \\\"pz0.2\\\": 0.763, \\\"pz0.3\\\": 0.78, \\\"pz0.4\\\": 0.874, \\\"pz0.5\\\": 0.857}, \\\"K4\\\": {\\\"pz0.1\\\": 1.542, \\\"pz0.2\\\": 1.684, \\\"pz0.3\\\": 1.121, \\\"pz0.4\\\": 1.082, \\\"pz0.5\\\": 1.063}}, \\\"hb_wins_K2\\\": \\\"all 5 P(Z=1) values (ratio 0.76-0.92)\\\", \\\"hb_wins_K4\\\": \\\"none (ratio 1.06-1.68)\\\", \\\"coverage_hb\\\": \\\"0.92-0.94 across all settings (near-nominal 0.95)\\\", \\\"coverage_naive\\\": \\\"0.65-0.79\\\", \\\"coverage_global\\\": \\\"0.79-0.90\\\", \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"Theorem 1 R²>0.976 universally — bias decomposition holds regardless of P(Z=1). EC-HTE RMSE advantage depends on K: K=2 HB wins everywhere, K=4 HB loses at n_expert=250 (insufficient per-subgroup minority data). Coverage: HB 0.92-0.94 near-nominal at all settings.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"Theorem 1 R²=0.976-0.998 across all P(Z=1)∈[0.1,0.5] × K∈{2,4} — bias decomposition holds universally, not dependent on class balance. EC-HTE RMSE: K=2 HB wins at all P(Z=1) (ratio 0.76-0.92); K=4 HB loses at n_expert=250 (ratio 1.06-1.68) due to 250/4=62.5 experts per subgroup being insufficient when minority class is small. Coverage: HB maintains 0.92-0.94 (near-nominal) while naive 0.65-0.79 and global 0.79-0.90 undercover. Directly addresses M2: A3 (class balance) affects RMSE but NOT Theorem 1 validity.\\\"}, \\\"exp-018\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:42:13.498Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:54:49.523Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"EC-HTE bias reduction persists under multiplicative CATE DGP (Y(1)=Y(0)*exp(tau(Z))), demonstrating robustness beyond additive treatment effect models\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-001\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"n_mc\\\": 50, \\\"config\\\": \\\"K=2/extreme/n_expert=500/multiplicative\\\", \\\"dgp\\\": \\\"Y(1) = Y(0) * exp(tau(Z)), tau(Z=0)=0, tau(Z=1)=0.3\\\", \\\"results\\\": {\\\"oracle\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.031, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.038, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.96}, \\\"ec_hte\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.054, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.068, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.955}, \\\"global_corrected\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.11, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.124, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.54}, \\\"naive\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.135, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.142, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.435}}, \\\"ec_hte_rmse_reduction_vs_naive\\\": \\\"52%\\\", \\\"ec_hte_coverage\\\": 0.955, \\\"vs_exp001_additive\\\": \\\"EC-HTE RMSE 0.068 (multiplicative) vs 0.091 (additive, same config) — actually better due to larger true CATEs making relative bias smaller\\\", \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"EC-HTE robust to non-additive treatment effects. 52% RMSE reduction vs naive, 95.5% coverage (target 95%). Answers Reviewer Q3.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"EC-HTE robust under multiplicative CATE DGP: RMSE 0.068 (52% reduction vs naive 0.142), coverage 0.955 (nominal 0.95). Global correction inadequate (coverage 0.54). Answers Reviewer Q3 on non-additive DGP robustness.\\\"}, \\\"exp-017\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:42:12.809Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:53:56.536Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"EC-HTE corrected pseudo-outcomes are compatible with R-learner, achieving similar bias reduction as with CausalForest\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-011\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"n_mc\\\": 50, \\\"config\\\": \\\"K=2/extreme/n_expert=500\\\", \\\"rlearner_results\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.121, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.139, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.47}, \\\"global\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.098, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.123, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.71}, \\\"ec_hte\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.013, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.08, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.92}, \\\"oracle\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.006, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.056, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.94}}, \\\"causal_forest_comparison\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.125, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.143, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.46}, \\\"global\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.104, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.129, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.65}, \\\"ec_hte\\\": {\\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.008, \\\"rmse\\\": 0.082, \\\"coverage\\\": 0.91}}, \\\"ec_hte_bias_reduction_vs_naive\\\": \\\"9.3x (R-learner) vs 15.6x (CausalForest)\\\", \\\"pattern_consistent\\\": true, \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"EC-HTE correction generalizes across CATE estimators. R-learner ec_hte achieves |bias|=0.013 (vs naive 0.121), 92% coverage — closely matching CausalForest pattern.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"EC-HTE correction generalizes to R-learner: |Bias| 0.121→0.013 (89% reduction), coverage 0.47→0.92. Consistent with CausalForest results (|Bias|=0.008, coverage 0.91). Validates estimator-agnostic upstream correction.\\\"}, \\\"exp-016\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:19:22.463Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:42:08.581Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"EC-HTE succeeds under realistic asymmetric CMs when worst-subgroup FNR is well below 0.5 — using gpt-4o (or best-FNR model from exp-008) real per-subgroup FPR/FNR on TweetEval, HB correction outperforms global at n_expert≥250\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-015\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"model_selected\\\": \\\"gpt-3.5-turbo\\\", \\\"worst_subgroup_fnr\\\": 0.211, \\\"worst_subgroup_fpr\\\": 0.393, \\\"rmse_n100\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.127, \\\"global\\\": 0.265, \\\"hb\\\": 0.93}, \\\"rmse_n250\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.127, \\\"global\\\": 0.221, \\\"hb\\\": 0.527}, \\\"rmse_n500\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.127, \\\"global\\\": 0.199, \\\"hb\\\": 0.322}, \\\"coverage_n100\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.784, \\\"global\\\": 0.893, \\\"hb\\\": 0.769}, \\\"coverage_n250\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.784, \\\"global\\\": 0.922, \\\"hb\\\": 0.843}, \\\"coverage_n500\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.784, \\\"global\\\": 0.939, \\\"hb\\\": 0.894}, \\\"hb_worse_than_global_all_n\\\": true, \\\"vs_exp015\\\": \\\"exp-016 HB RMSE even worse than exp-015 despite lower FNR, because high FPR variance (0.14-0.39) makes subgroup CM estimation harder\\\", \\\"bright_spot\\\": \\\"S0/z=1 at n=500: HB |bias|=0.193 < Global |bias|=0.252 — one case where subgroup-specific correction helps\\\", \\\"root_cause\\\": \\\"High FPR variance across subgroups (0.143-0.393) + P(Z=1)=0.18 → insufficient Z=1 expert labels per subgroup. Lower FNR alone does not fix the fundamental sample-size constraint.\\\", \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"Neither low-FNR (gpt-3.5-turbo) nor low-FPR (GPT-4.1) real CMs enable EC-HTE to beat global. The bottleneck is P(Z=1)=0.18 creating too few positive-class expert labels per subgroup for reliable CM estimation.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"HB EC-HTE fails under gpt-3.5-turbo real asymmetric CMs despite worst-FNR=0.211 (<0.4). Root cause: FPR≈0.4 + P(Z=1)=0.18 class imbalance → mixing matrix ill-conditioned. Demonstrates that CM condition number alone is insufficient; class-prior-adjusted mixing matrix condition is the true diagnostic.\\\"}, \\\"exp-015\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T12:49:47.680Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T13:04:54.905Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"EC-HTE maintains bias correction under realistic asymmetric confusion matrices estimated from actual GPT-4.1 annotations on TweetEval (Δπ=0.098, FNR/FPR ratio up to 10×)\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp-003\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"n_mc\\\": 100, \\\"n_expert_grid\\\": [100, 250, 500], \\\"subgroups\\\": 4, \\\"real_cm_source\\\": \\\"exp-012 GPT-4.1 pilot\\\", \\\"cm_params\\\": {\\\"S0_False_medium\\\": {\\\"FPR\\\": 0.054, \\\"FNR\\\": 0.526, \\\"weight\\\": 0.38}, \\\"S1_False_short\\\": {\\\"FPR\\\": 0.101, \\\"FNR\\\": 0.3, \\\"weight\\\": 0.22}, \\\"S2_True_short\\\": {\\\"FPR\\\": 0.143, \\\"FNR\\\": 0.308, \\\"weight\\\": 0.14}, \\\"S3_True_medium\\\": {\\\"FPR\\\": 0.194, \\\"FNR\\\": 0.222, \\\"weight\\\": 0.25}}, \\\"delta_pi\\\": 0.098, \\\"P_Z1\\\": 0.18, \\\"rmse_n100\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.123, \\\"global\\\": 0.263, \\\"hb\\\": 0.784}, \\\"rmse_n250\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.123, \\\"global\\\": 0.232, \\\"hb\\\": 0.411}, \\\"rmse_n500\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.123, \\\"global\\\": 0.196, \\\"hb\\\": 0.26}, \\\"coverage_n100\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.868, \\\"global\\\": 0.922, \\\"hb\\\": 0.744}, \\\"coverage_n250\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.868, \\\"global\\\": 0.908, \\\"hb\\\": 0.828}, \\\"coverage_n500\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.873, \\\"global\\\": 0.885, \\\"hb\\\": 0.89}, \\\"hb_worse_than_global_all_n\\\": true, \\\"root_cause\\\": \\\"S0 FNR=0.526 makes CM row near-uniform [0.526, 0.474], inversion amplifies noise. Combined with P(Z=1)=0.18, too few Z=1 expert labels per subgroup for reliable per-subgroup CM estimation.\\\", \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"EC-HTE fails under realistic asymmetric CMs when any subgroup has FNR≈0.5 (near-random for positive class). This is consistent with exp-004 (FNR=53%) and exp-013 (boundary at n_expert/K=12.5). Identifies classifier quality threshold as honest limitation.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"negative: EC-HTE fails under realistic asymmetric CMs from actual GPT-4.1 annotations. HB RMSE 1.3-3.0× worse than global across all n_expert. Root cause: S0 (38% weight) FNR=0.526 → CM row near-uniform → inversion amplifies noise. Converges with exp-004 (FNR=53%) and exp-013 (n_expert/K boundary). Establishes classifier quality threshold as second applicability condition alongside budget threshold.\\\"}, \\\"exp-008\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T08:06:37.084Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T11:51:10.336Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Subgroup-specific misclassification heterogeneity (Δπ > 0.05) persists across model generations and scales within the OpenAI family (from GPT-3.5 to GPT-5.4, spanning three model generations), demonstrating the bias mechanism is a structural property of LLM annotation rather than an artifact of a specific model version\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"models_completed\\\": [\\\"gpt-3.5-turbo\\\", \\\"gpt-4.1\\\", \\\"gpt-4o\\\", \\\"gpt-4o-mini\\\", \\\"gpt-4-turbo\\\", \\\"gpt-5.4\\\"], \\\"total_models\\\": 6, \\\"proxy_provider\\\": \\\"OpenAI only\\\", \\\"gpt5x_model_used\\\": \\\"gpt-5.4\\\", \\\"gpt55_failed\\\": \\\"reasoning tokens consume max_tokens budget, too slow (~1min/call)\\\", \\\"filtered_analysis\\\": {\\\"excluded_subgroups\\\": [\\\"False_long (n=6)\\\", \\\"True_long (n=4)\\\"], \\\"min_n_threshold\\\": 10, \\\"retained_subgroups\\\": [\\\"False_medium (n=187)\\\", \\\"False_short (n=109)\\\", \\\"True_medium (n=125)\\\", \\\"True_short (n=69)\\\"]}, \\\"effective_delta_pi\\\": {\\\"gpt-3.5-turbo\\\": 0.198, \\\"gpt-4-turbo\\\": 0.149, \\\"gpt-4.1\\\": 0.098, \\\"gpt-4o\\\": 0.08, \\\"gpt-4o-mini\\\": 0.136, \\\"gpt-5.4\\\": 0.114}, \\\"all_effective_delta_pi_above_005\\\": true, \\\"worst_subgroup_consensus\\\": \\\"True_medium (5/6 models)\\\", \\\"spearman_significant_pairs\\\": [\\\"gpt-4-turbo vs gpt-4o (ρ=1.000)\\\", \\\"gpt-4.1 vs gpt-4o-mini (ρ=1.000)\\\", \\\"gpt-4.1 vs gpt-5.4 (ρ=1.000)\\\", \\\"gpt-4o-mini vs gpt-5.4 (ρ=1.000)\\\"], \\\"spearman_note\\\": \\\"Only 4 subgroups after filtering, limited statistical power (most p>0.20)\\\", \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"All 6 models (GPT-3.5 through GPT-5.4, three generations) show effective Δπ > 0.05 after excluding n<10 subgroups. Heterogeneity is structural, not model-specific.\\\"}}, \\\"exp-004\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T08:06:35.407Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T11:02:43.280Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"On real hate speech data (UC Berkeley), LLM annotation of dehumanization level exhibits text-feature-based misclassification heterogeneity (e.g., by target group type, text length, presence of explicit language), and EC-HTE subgroup-stratified correction produces qualitatively different CATE distributions compared to global correction — demonstrating the practical relevance of the bias mechanism with text-feature subgroups consistent with the TweetEval experiment design\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"dataset\\\": \\\"UC Berkeley Measuring Hate Speech\\\", \\\"n_comments\\\": 2496, \\\"valid_annotations\\\": 2496, \\\"subgroups\\\": {\\\"plat0_long\\\": 624, \\\"plat0_short\\\": 624, \\\"plat23_long\\\": 624, \\\"plat23_short\\\": 624}, \\\"subgroup_type\\\": \\\"text features (platform_group × text_length)\\\", \\\"delta_pi\\\": 0.117, \\\"global_FPR\\\": 0.139, \\\"global_FNR\\\": 0.533, \\\"misclass_rates\\\": {\\\"plat0_long\\\": 0.266, \\\"plat0_short\\\": 0.337, \\\"plat23_long\\\": 0.303, \\\"plat23_short\\\": 0.383}, \\\"avg_abs_bias\\\": {\\\"naive\\\": 0.21, \\\"global\\\": 0.273, \\\"ec_hte\\\": 0.313}, \\\"cate_comparison\\\": \\\"EC-HTE does not outperform global on avg |bias| — high FNR (~53%) and wide CIs limit correction effectiveness on this dataset\\\", \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"Δπ=0.117 confirms real-world misclassification heterogeneity across text-feature subgroups. High global FNR (53%) indicates LLM severely under-classifies dehumanization. CATE correction comparison inconclusive due to wide bootstrap CIs.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"Δπ=0.117 confirms subgroup-varying misclassification in real hate speech data. However, EC-HTE correction fails (|bias| 0.313 > Global 0.273 > Naive 0.210) due to extreme baseline FNR=53.3%. This identifies a classifier quality threshold as a boundary condition for EC-HTE: when P(Z_hat=1|Z_true=1) ≈ 0.5 (near-random for positive class), confusion matrix inversion amplifies noise regardless of stratification.\\\"}, \\\"exp-010\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T09:57:20.485Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T10:18:33.801Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"EC-HTE with HB partial pooling degrades gracefully under subgroup misspecification: mild noise (10%) causes minimal performance loss due to shrinkage toward global, moderate noise (25%) reduces but preserves advantage over global correction, and severe noise (50%) may eliminate EC-HTE's advantage — demonstrating both the method's robustness range and its honest limitation\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"n_mc\\\": 100, \\\"configs\\\": 2, \\\"noise_levels\\\": [0, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5], \\\"total_runs\\\": 800, \\\"runtime_sec\\\": 15, \\\"k2_coverage_naive\\\": [0.875, 0.877, 0.89, 0.897], \\\"k2_coverage_hb\\\": [0.958, 0.948, 0.95, 0.945], \\\"k2_coverage_global\\\": [0.925, 0.935, 0.932, 0.945], \\\"k4_coverage_naive\\\": [0.904, 0.916, 0.909, 0.919], \\\"k4_coverage_hb\\\": [0.956, 0.958, 0.96, 0.946], \\\"k4_coverage_global\\\": [0.93, 0.953, 0.948, 0.944], \\\"hb_beats_mle_all_configs\\\": true, \\\"hb_improvement_range\\\": \\\"+0.1% to +0.9% RMSE\\\", \\\"graceful_convergence_at_50pct\\\": true, \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"Coverage is the decisive metric: naive 87-92%, EC-HTE 95-96% across all noise levels. No catastrophic failure. HB shrinkage provides automatic safeguard — converges to global at 50% noise.\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"EC-HTE maintains near-nominal CI coverage (≥94.5%) across all subgroup noise levels (0-50%), confirming robustness of inferential validity under misspecified subgroup structure. RMSE advantage is not observed at n_expert=500 (convergence zone per exp-006), but coverage is the decisive metric for statistical inference. At p_flip=0.50, EC-HTE gracefully converges to global correction. HB and MLE perform comparably at n=500 (expected per exp-006).\\\"}, \\\"exp-014\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T07:27:32.305Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-21T07:45:15.046Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"The bias decomposition product structure (delta_s * delta_tau) persists under asymmetric misclassification (FPR != FNR), and EC-HTE subgroup-stratified correction remains effective — the symmetric assumption A2 is sufficient for the qualitative insight but not necessary for EC-HTE to work\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 3, \\\"asymmetric_configs\\\": [\\\"balanced(FPR=0.15,FNR=0.25)\\\", \\\"fpr_dominant(FPR=0.30,FNR=0.10)\\\", \\\"fnr_dominant(FPR=0.10,FNR=0.30)\\\"], \\\"delta_tau_grid\\\": [0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5], \\\"n_mc\\\": 100, \\\"N\\\": 5000, \\\"n_expert\\\": 250, \\\"r2_analytic_vs_mc\\\": 0.992, \\\"product_structure_cv\\\": \\\"~1e-16 (exact)\\\", \\\"product_structure_confirmed\\\": true, \\\"a2_not_required\\\": true, \\\"ec_hte_avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.006, \\\"ec_hte_avg_rmse\\\": 0.033, \\\"ec_hte_avg_coverage\\\": 0.9, \\\"global_avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.025, \\\"global_avg_rmse\\\": 0.04, \\\"global_avg_coverage\\\": 0.78, \\\"naive_avg_abs_bias\\\": 0.025, \\\"naive_avg_rmse\\\": 0.039, \\\"naive_avg_coverage\\\": 0.87, \\\"bias_reduction_vs_global\\\": \\\"4.2x\\\", \\\"coverage_improvement_vs_global\\\": \\\"+12pp\\\", \\\"latex_derivation_included\\\": true, \\\"key_finding\\\": \\\"Product structure bias = Δτ·[B_s]_{z,1} holds exactly under asymmetric misclassification. High-Δτ subgroup (True_short) RMSE wins at n≥500. Aggregate RMSE loss driven by low-Δτ subgroups where stratified variance cost exceeds small bias gain.\"}}, \"exp-022\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T19:43:54.613Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T19:44:39.795Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"EC-HTE's hierarchical Bayes confusion matrix estimation is insensitive to Gamma hyperprior specification: varying shape∈{1,2,3,5,10} and scale∈{0.5,1,2} produces <10% RMSE variation, confirming the method is not driven by prior choice\", \"parent\": \"exp-001\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"rmse_range_reasonable\": \"6.0%\", \"rmse_range_all\": \"17.3%\", \"gamma31_rmse\": 0.0841, \"gamma31_rank\": \"8/15\", \"coverage_range\": \"0.925-0.938\", \"hb_beats_mle\": \"9/15\", \"best_config\": \"Gamma(1,0.5)\", \"best_rmse\": 0.0825}}, \"exp-021\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T17:50:05.855Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T17:50:34.986Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Table 1 RMSE results are stable under doubling of MC seeds (50→100); median cell change <5% confirms 50-seed results are not artifacts of sampling variance\", \"parent\": \"exp-001\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"total_cells_compared\": 94, \"median_delta_pct\": 3.2, \"max_delta_pct\": 25.5, \"cells_under_5pct\": \"79/94 (84%)\", \"cells_under_10pct\": \"93/94 (99%)\", \"outlier\": \"K=4/extreme/n=100/HB base (-25.5%, known numerically unstable)\", \"method_note\": \"EB instead of Gibbs (<0.002 CM difference, 10s vs 114min)\"}, \"notes\": \"100-seed stability test for Table 1. 84% cells <5% change, 99% <10%. Only outlier: K=4/extreme/n=100/HB base (-25.5%) — known numerically unstable cell where HB overshoots (RMSE 0.71→0.53, still bad but less bad). Used EB estimator for speed (10s vs 114min Gibbs); EB-Gibbs CM difference <0.002. Non-HB methods identical. Addresses RD2-M4.\"}, \"exp-020\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T16:14:37.738Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T16:15:14.257Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"κ=100 condition number guard provides sufficient safety margin for EC-HTE; empirical sweep will show guard rarely triggers under moderate misclassification and the exact threshold is not critical for well-conditioned problems\", \"parent\": \"exp-001\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"max_cond_number\": 1.89, \"mean_cond_number\": 1.446, \"p99_cond_number\": 1.893, \"guard_trigger_rate_all_kappa\": 0, \"rmse_hb\": 0.0759, \"rmse_global\": 0.0765, \"rmse_ratio\": 1.048, \"numerical_instability_rate\": 0, \"safety_margin_at_kappa100\": \"53x\", \"stress_test_near_random_max_cond\": 1125.3, \"stress_test_near_random_triggers_kappa100\": true}, \"notes\": \"κ sweep across {10,25,50,100,200,500,1000}: guard never triggers in moderate regime (max cond=1.89, 53× below κ=100). All κ values produce identical RMSE (HB=0.076, Global=0.077). Zero numerical instability. Stress test: guard only activates with near-random labeling (π_s≈0.4-0.5, n_expert=50, max cond=1125). κ=100 validated as safe default. Adaptive formula κ=10·√(n_expert/K) recovers κ=100 for this regime. Addresses RD2-W4.\"}, \"exp-019\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T15:38:56.994Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T15:39:34.345Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Theorem 1 bias decomposition R² remains high (>0.95) across P(Z=1) range [0.1, 0.5], and EC-HTE RMSE advantage over global persists but degrades at low P(Z=1) due to insufficient minority-class expert labels\", \"parent\": \"exp-001\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"theorem1_r2\": {\"K2\": {\"pz0.1\": 0.998, \"pz0.2\": 0.989, \"pz0.3\": 0.994, \"pz0.4\": 0.995, \"pz0.5\": 0.995}, \"K4\": {\"pz0.1\": 0.984, \"pz0.2\": 0.976, \"pz0.3\": 0.985, \"pz0.4\": 0.984, \"pz0.5\": 0.992}}, \"theorem1_r2_range\": \"0.976-0.998 (all >0.95)\", \"rmse_ratio_hb_over_global\": {\"K2\": {\"pz0.1\": 0.92, \"pz0.2\": 0.763, \"pz0.3\": 0.78, \"pz0.4\": 0.874, \"pz0.5\": 0.857}, \"K4\": {\"pz0.1\": 1.542, \"pz0.2\": 1.684, \"pz0.3\": 1.121, \"pz0.4\": 1.082, \"pz0.5\": 1.063}}, \"hb_wins_K2\": \"all 5 P(Z=1) values (ratio 0.76-0.92)\", \"hb_wins_K4\": \"none (ratio 1.06-1.68)\", \"coverage_hb\": \"0.92-0.94 across all settings (near-nominal 0.95)\", \"coverage_naive\": \"0.65-0.79\", \"coverage_global\": \"0.79-0.90\", \"key_finding\": \"Theorem 1 R²>0.976 universally — bias decomposition holds regardless of P(Z=1). EC-HTE RMSE advantage depends on K: K=2 HB wins everywhere, K=4 HB loses at n_expert=250 (insufficient per-subgroup minority data). Coverage: HB 0.92-0.94 near-nominal at all settings.\"}, \"notes\": \"Theorem 1 R²=0.976-0.998 across all P(Z=1)∈[0.1,0.5] × K∈{2,4} — bias decomposition holds universally, not dependent on class balance. EC-HTE RMSE: K=2 HB wins at all P(Z=1) (ratio 0.76-0.92); K=4 HB loses at n_expert=250 (ratio 1.06-1.68) due to 250/4=62.5 experts per subgroup being insufficient when minority class is small. Coverage: HB maintains 0.92-0.94 (near-nominal) while naive 0.65-0.79 and global 0.79-0.90 undercover. Directly addresses M2: A3 (class balance) affects RMSE but NOT Theorem 1 validity.\"}, \"exp-018\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T13:42:13.498Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T13:54:49.523Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"EC-HTE bias reduction persists under multiplicative CATE DGP (Y(1)=Y(0)*exp(tau(Z))), demonstrating robustness beyond additive treatment effect models\", \"parent\": \"exp-001\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"n_mc\": 50, \"config\": \"K=2/extreme/n_expert=500/multiplicative\", \"dgp\": \"Y(1) = Y(0) * exp(tau(Z)), tau(Z=0)=0, tau(Z=1)=0.3\", \"results\": {\"oracle\": {\"avg_abs_bias\": 0.031, \"rmse\": 0.038, \"coverage\": 0.96}, \"ec_hte\": {\"avg_abs_bias\": 0.054, \"rmse\": 0.068, \"coverage\": 0.955}, \"global_corrected\": {\"avg_abs_bias\": 0.11, \"rmse\": 0.124, \"coverage\": 0.54}, \"naive\": {\"avg_abs_bias\": 0.135, \"rmse\": 0.142, \"coverage\": 0.435}}, \"ec_hte_rmse_reduction_vs_naive\": \"52%\", \"ec_hte_coverage\": 0.955, \"vs_exp001_additive\": \"EC-HTE RMSE 0.068 (multiplicative) vs 0.091 (additive, same config) — actually better due to larger true CATEs making relative bias smaller\", \"key_finding\": \"EC-HTE robust to non-additive treatment effects. 52% RMSE reduction vs naive, 95.5% coverage (target 95%). Root cause: FPR≈0.4 + P(Z=1)=0.18 class imbalance → mixing matrix ill-conditioned. Demonstrates that CM condition number alone is insufficient; class-prior-adjusted mixing matrix condition is the true diagnostic.\"}, \"exp-015\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T12:49:47.680Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T13:04:54.905Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"EC-HTE maintains bias correction under realistic asymmetric confusion matrices estimated from actual GPT-4.1 annotations on TweetEval (Δπ=0.098, FNR/FPR ratio up to 10×)\", \"parent\": \"exp-003\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\", \"metrics\": {\"n_mc\": 100, \"n_expert_grid\": [100, 250, 500], \"subgroups\": 4, \"real_cm_source\": \"exp-012 GPT-4.1 pilot\", \"cm_params\": {\"S0_False_medium\": {\"FPR\": 0.054, \"FNR\": 0.526, \"weight\": 0.38}, \"S1_False_short\": {\"FPR\": 0.101, \"FNR\": 0.3, \"weight\": 0.22}, \"S2_True_short\": {\"FPR\": 0.143, \"FNR\": 0.308, \"weight\": 0.14}, \"S3_True_medium\": {\"FPR\": 0.194, \"FNR\": 0.222, \"weight\": 0.25}}, \"delta_pi\": 0.098, \"P_Z1\": 0.18, \"rmse_n100\": {\"naive\": 0.123, \"global\": 0.263, \"hb\": 0.784}, \"rmse_n250\": {\"naive\": 0.123, \"global\": 0.232, \"hb\": 0.411}, \"rmse_n500\": {\"naive\": 0.123, \"global\": 0.196, \"hb\": 0.26}, \"coverage_n100\": {\"naive\": 0.868, \"global\": 0.922, \"hb\": 0.744}, \"coverage_n250\": {\"naive\": 0.868, \"global\": 0.908, \"hb\": 0.828}, \"coverage_n500\": {\"naive\": 0.873, \"global\": 0.885, \"hb\": 0.89}, \"hb_worse_than_global_all_n\": true, \"root_cause\": \"S0 FNR=0.526 makes CM row near-uniform [0.526, 0.474], inversion amplifies noise. HB RMSE 1.3-3.0× worse than global across all n_expert. Root cause: S0 (38% weight) FNR=0.526 → CM row near-uniform → inversion amplifies noise. Converges with exp-004 (FNR=53%) and exp-013 (n_expert/K boundary). Establishes classifier quality threshold as second applicability condition alongside budget threshold.\"}, \"exp-008\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T08:06:37.084Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T11:51:10.336Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Subgroup-specific misclassification heterogeneity (Δπ > 0.05) persists across model generations and scales within the OpenAI family (from GPT-3.5 to GPT-5.4, spanning three model generations), demonstrating the bias mechanism is a structural property of LLM annotation rather than an artifact of a specific model version\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"models_completed\": [\"gpt-3.5-turbo\", \"gpt-4.1\", \"gpt-4o\", \"gpt-4o-mini\", \"gpt-4-turbo\", \"gpt-5.4\"], \"total_models\": 6, \"proxy_provider\": \"OpenAI only\", \"gpt5x_model_used\": \"gpt-5.4\", \"gpt55_failed\": \"reasoning tokens consume max_tokens budget, too slow (~1min/call)\", \"filtered_analysis\": {\"excluded_subgroups\": [\"False_long (n=6)\", \"True_long (n=4)\"], \"min_n_threshold\": 10, \"retained_subgroups\": [\"False_medium (n=187)\", \"False_short (n=109)\", \"True_medium (n=125)\", \"True_short (n=69)\"]}, \"effective_delta_pi\": {\"gpt-3.5-turbo\": 0.198, \"gpt-4-turbo\": 0.149, \"gpt-4.1\": 0.098, \"gpt-4o\": 0.08, \"gpt-4o-mini\": 0.136, \"gpt-5.4\": 0.114}, \"all_effective_delta_pi_above_005\": true, \"worst_subgroup_consensus\": \"True_medium (5/6 models)\", \"spearman_significant_pairs\": [\"gpt-4-turbo vs gpt-4o (ρ=1.000)\", \"gpt-4.1 vs gpt-4o-mini (ρ=1.000)\", \"gpt-4.1 vs gpt-5.4 (ρ=1.000)\", \"gpt-4o-mini vs gpt-5.4 (ρ=1.000)\"], \"spearman_note\": \"Only 4 subgroups after filtering, limited statistical power (most p>0.20)\", \"key_finding\": \"All 6 models (GPT-3.5 through GPT-5.4, three generations) show effective Δπ > 0.05 after excluding n<10 subgroups. Heterogeneity is structural, not model-specific.\"}}, \"exp-004\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T08:06:35.407Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T11:02:43.280Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"On real hate speech data (UC Berkeley), LLM annotation of dehumanization level exhibits text-feature-based misclassification heterogeneity (e.g., by target group type, text length, presence of explicit language), and EC-HTE subgroup-stratified correction produces qualitatively different CATE distributions compared to global correction — demonstrating the practical relevance of the bias mechanism with text-feature subgroups consistent with the TweetEval experiment design\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"dataset\": \"UC Berkeley Measuring Hate Speech\", \"n_comments\": 2496, \"valid_annotations\": 2496, \"subgroups\": {\"plat0_long\": 624, \"plat0_short\": 624, \"plat23_long\": 624, \"plat23_short\": 624}, \"subgroup_type\": \"text features (platform_group × text_length)\", \"delta_pi\": 0.117, \"global_FPR\": 0.139, \"global_FNR\": 0.533, \"misclass_rates\": {\"plat0_long\": 0.266, \"plat0_short\": 0.337, \"plat23_long\": 0.303, \"plat23_short\": 0.383}, \"avg_abs_bias\": {\"naive\": 0.21, \"global\": 0.273, \"ec_hte\": 0.313}, \"cate_comparison\": \"EC-HTE does not outperform global on avg |bias| — high FNR (~53%) and wide CIs limit correction effectiveness on this dataset\", \"key_finding\": \"Δπ=0.117 confirms real-world misclassification heterogeneity across text-feature subgroups. High global FNR (53%) indicates LLM severely under-classifies dehumanization. CATE correction comparison inconclusive due to wide bootstrap CIs.\"}, \"notes\": \"Δπ=0.117 confirms subgroup-varying misclassification in real hate speech data. However, EC-HTE correction fails (|bias| 0.313 > Global 0.273 > Naive 0.210) due to extreme baseline FNR=53.3%. This identifies a classifier quality threshold as a boundary condition for EC-HTE: when P(Z_hat=1|Z_true=1) ≈ 0.5 (near-random for positive class), confusion matrix inversion amplifies noise regardless of stratification.\"}, \"exp-010\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T09:57:20.485Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T10:18:33.801Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"EC-HTE with HB partial pooling degrades gracefully under subgroup misspecification: mild noise (10%) causes minimal performance loss due to shrinkage toward global, moderate noise (25%) reduces but preserves advantage over global correction, and severe noise (50%) may eliminate EC-HTE's advantage — demonstrating both the method's robustness range and its honest limitation\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"n_mc\": 100, \"configs\": 2, \"noise_levels\": [0, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5], \"total_runs\": 800, \"runtime_sec\": 15, \"k2_coverage_naive\": [0.875, 0.877, 0.89, 0.897], \"k2_coverage_hb\": [0.958, 0.948, 0.95, 0.945], \"k2_coverage_global\": [0.925, 0.935, 0.932, 0.945], \"k4_coverage_naive\": [0.904, 0.916, 0.909, 0.919], \"k4_coverage_hb\": [0.956, 0.958, 0.96, 0.946], \"k4_coverage_global\": [0.93, 0.953, 0.948, 0.944], \"hb_beats_mle_all_configs\": true, \"hb_improvement_range\": \"+0.1% to +0.9% RMSE\", \"graceful_convergence_at_50pct\": true, \"key_finding\": \"Coverage is the decisive metric: naive 87-92%, EC-HTE 95-96% across all noise levels. No catastrophic failure. HB shrinkage provides automatic safeguard — converges to global at 50% noise.\"}, \"notes\": \"EC-HTE maintains near-nominal CI coverage (≥94.5%) across all subgroup noise levels (0-50%), confirming robustness of inferential validity under misspecified subgroup structure. RMSE advantage is not observed at n_expert=500 (convergence zone per exp-006), but coverage is the decisive metric for statistical inference. At p_flip=0.50, EC-HTE gracefully converges to global correction. HB and MLE perform comparably at n=500 (expected per exp-006).\"}, \"exp-014\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T07:27:32.305Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T07:45:15.046Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"The bias decomposition product structure (delta_s * delta_tau) persists under asymmetric misclassification (FPR != FNR), and EC-HTE subgroup-stratified correction remains effective — the symmetric assumption A2 is sufficient for the qualitative insight but not necessary for EC-HTE to work\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"configs\": 3, \"asymmetric_configs\": [\"balanced(FPR=0.15,FNR=0.25)\", \"fpr_dominant(FPR=0.30,FNR=0.10)\", \"fnr_dominant(FPR=0.10,FNR=0.30)\"], \"delta_tau_grid\": [0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5], \"n_mc\": 100, \"N\": 5000, \"n_expert\": 250, \"r2_analytic_vs_mc\": 0.992, \"product_structure_cv\": \"~1e-16 (exact)\", \"product_structure_confirmed\": true, \"a2_not_required\": true, \"ec_hte_avg_abs_bias\": 0.006, \"ec_hte_avg_rmse\": 0.033, \"ec_hte_avg_coverage\": 0.9, \"global_avg_abs_bias\": 0.025, \"global_avg_rmse\": 0.04, \"global_avg_coverage\": 0.78, \"naive_avg_abs_bias\": 0.025, \"naive_avg_rmse\": 0.039, \"naive_avg_coverage\": 0.87, \"bias_reduction_vs_global\": \"4.2x\", \"coverage_improvement_vs_global\": \"+12pp\", \"latex_derivation_included\": true, \"key_finding\": \"Product structure bias = Δτ·[B_s]_{z,1} holds exactly under asymmetric misclassification. A2 (symmetric) assumption is NOT required. 1	# exp-023: Civil Comments Semi-Synthetic Validation — Comprehensive Analysis
```
```text
2	
3	## 1. Dataset & Subgroup Design
4	
5	**Dataset**: Civil Comments (Google, toxicity detection), 3000 stratified samples (30% toxic)
6	**Subgroups**: text_length_bin × has_question → K=4 (S0: long+no_q, S1: long+q, S2: short+no_q, S3: short+q)
7	**Subgroup weights**: S0=0.388, S1=0.114, S2=0.337, S3=0.161
8	**P(Z=1)**: 0.300
9	
10	## 2. LLM Annotation Confusion Matrices
11	
12	Annotated with 3 LLMs via OpenAI-compatible proxy. 800–900 valid annotations per model.
13	
14	### Per-subgroup error rates
15	
16	| Model | Metric | S0 | S1 | S2 | S3 | Δ (max−min) |
17	|-------|--------|-----|-----|-----|-----|------------|
18	| gpt-4o | FPR | 0.249 | 0.157 | 0.213 | 0.341 | 0.184 |
19	| gpt-4o | FNR | 0.160 | 0.263 | 0.308 | 0.328 | 0.168 |
20	| gpt-4o-mini | FPR | 0.517 | 0.541 | 0.419 | 0.610 | 0.191 |
21	| gpt-4o-mini | FNR | 0.052 | 0.105 | 0.074 | 0.160 | 0.108 |
22	| gpt-3.5-turbo | FPR | 0.400 | 0.473 | 0.374 | 0.468 | 0.099 |
23	| gpt-3.5-turbo | FNR | 0.139 | 0.211 | 0.173 | 0.240 | 0.101 |
24	
25	### Key Finding 1: Cross-subgroup misclassification heterogeneity confirmed
26	
27	All 3 models show **Δπ > 0.05** across subgroups:
28	- gpt-4o: Δ_FPR=0.184, Δ_FNR=0.168 (overall Δπ=0.160)
29	- gpt-4o-mini: Δ_FPR=0.191, Δ_FNR=0.108 (overall Δπ=0.140)
30	- gpt-3.5-turbo: Δ_FPR=0.099, Δ_FNR=0.101 (overall Δπ=0.113)
31	
32	This validates the paper's core premise: LLM misclassification is structurally heterogeneous across text-feature-defined subgroups, and this holds on a second NLP dataset beyond TweetEval.
33	
34	### Key Finding 2: Severe FPR/FNR asymmetry
35	
36	gpt-4o-mini and gpt-3.5-turbo show highly asymmetric confusion matrices:
37	- gpt-4o-mini: FPR ≈ 0.42–0.61 while FNR ≈ 0.05–0.16 (over-predicts toxicity)
38	- gpt-3.5-turbo: FPR ≈ 0.37–0.47 while FNR ≈ 0.14–0.24
39	
40	This asymmetry causes mixing matrix ill-conditioning (rows become near-uniform when FPR ≈ 0.5), which amplifies estimation noise under matrix inversion.
41	
42	## 3. Semi-Synthetic DGP Results
43	
44	### 3a. Asymmetric CMs (real LLM confusion matrices)
45	
46	**DGP**: N=5000, τ(z=0)=0.10, τ(z=1)=0.40, MC=50, uniform CATE (no per-subgroup variation)
47	
48	Results at n_expert=250:
49	
50	| Model | Method | |Bias| | RMSE | Coverage |
51	|-------|--------|-------|------|----------|
52	| gpt-3.5-turbo | naive | 0.1194 | 0.1374 | 0.708 |
53	| gpt-3.5-turbo | global_corrected | 0.1578 | 0.1963 | 0.948 |
54	| gpt-3.5-turbo | hb_ec_hte | 0.3188 | 0.4811 | 0.938 |
55	| gpt-4o | naive | 0.1015 | 0.1222 | 0.820 |
56	| gpt-4o | global_corrected | 0.1425 | 0.1745 | 0.945 |
57	| gpt-4o | hb_ec_hte | 0.2473 | 0.3629 | 0.912 |
58	| gpt-4o-mini | naive | 0.1303 | 0.1475 | 0.635 |
59	| gpt-4o-mini | global_corrected | 0.1581 | 0.1986 | 0.948 |
60	| gpt-4o-mini | hb_ec_hte | 0.2842 | 0.4201 | 0.938 |
61	
62	**Result**: EC-HTE has 2–3× worse RMSE than global correction with asymmetric CMs. Consistent with exp-015/016 failure analysis — asymmetric CMs with FPR ≈ 0.5 cause ill-conditioning.

86

88	|----------|--------|-------|------|----------|
89	| 250 | oracle | 0.0705 | 0.0897 | 0.960 |
90	| 250 | naive | 0.0946 | 0.1126 | 0.843 |
91	| 250 | global_corrected | 0.1318 | 0.1678 | 0.948 |
92	| 250 | hb_ec_hte | 0.1874 | 0.2857 | 0.938 |
93	| 500 | naive | 0.0946 | 0.1126 | 0.843 |
94	| 500 | global_corrected | 0.1229 | 0.1538 | 0.955 |
95	| 500 | hb_ec_hte | 0.1532 | 0.2134 | 0.952 |
96	
97	#### gpt-4o-mini rates (π = {0.350, 0.452, 0.312, 0.433})
98	
99	| n_expert | Method | |Bias| | RMSE | Coverage |
100	|----------|--------|-------|------|----------|
101	| 250 | oracle | 0.0705 | 0.0897 | 0.960 |
102	| 250 | naive | 0.1197 | 0.1372 | 0.720 |
103	| 250 | global_corrected | 0.2315 | 0.2862 | 0.975 |
104	| 250 | hb_ec_hte | 0.4410 | 0.7134 | 0.968 |
105	| 500 | naive | 0.1197 | 0.1372 | 0.720 |
106	| 500 | global_corrected | 0.2078 | 0.2539 | 0.970 |
107	| 500 | hb_ec_hte | 0.4670 | 0.7424 | 0.970 |
108	
109	## 4. Key Findings
110	
111	### Finding 1: Δπ > 0.05 confirmed across all models ✓
112	Civil Comments reproduces the subgroup-heterogeneous misclassification structure found in TweetEval. This holds for GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, and GPT-3.5-turbo across text-feature subgroups.
113	
114	### Finding 2: Coverage is EC-HTE's value proposition
115	Naive estimation has severely degraded coverage when misclassification is moderate-to-high:
116	- gpt-4o-mini rates: Naive coverage = 0.720 (target: 0.95)
117	- gpt-3.5-turbo rates: Naive coverage = 0.768
118	- gpt-4o rates: Naive coverage = 0.843
119	
120	All correction methods restore coverage to ~0.93–0.97. EC-HTE and global correction are both effective here.
121	
122	### Finding 3: RMSE ordering is Naive < Global < EC-HTE across all settings
123	This is the honest empirical result: **no correction method achieves lower RMSE than naive** at any sample size tested.
124	
125	The RMSE penalty arises from matrix inversion amplifying estimation noise. Even EC-HTE's partial pooling (which does reduce variance relative to stratified MLE in most cases) cannot overcome this.
126	
127	This ordering is also observed on TweetEval (exp-003), so it is not specific to Civil Comments.
128	
129	### Finding 4: EC-HTE ≈ Stratified MLE (partial pooling has marginal effect at K=4)
130	EC-HTE/StratMLE RMSE ratio ranges from 0.83 to 1.15 across settings. Partial pooling helps but the improvement is modest at K=4 because there is limited cross-subgroup information to borrow.
131	
132	## 5. Interpretation
133	
134	The results are consistent across both datasets (TweetEval and Civil Comments):
135	
136	1. **Misclassification heterogeneity is a real structural property** of LLM annotations — not an artifact of one dataset.
137	
138	2. **The bias-variance trade-off favors naive estimation in RMSE** when misclassification rates are moderate (π < 0.25). The bias introduced by misclassification is smaller than the variance introduced by matrix inversion correction.
139	
140	3. **Correction methods win on coverage/calibration**: When the researcher needs valid confidence intervals (not just point estimates), correction is essential. Naive CIs have severe under-coverage for the Z=1 group.
141	
142	4. **Asymmetric CMs (FPR ≠ FNR) worsen the problem**: Real LLM CMs are highly asymmetric, causing additional ill-conditioning beyond the symmetric case.
143	
144	## 6. What This Means for the Paper
145	
146	For Reviewer C1's concern about generalization beyond TweetEval:
147	
148	**Supported claims**:
149	- Subgroup-heterogeneous misclassification is a structural property of LLM annotations (confirmed on 2nd dataset)
150	- EC-HTE provides calibrated inference (valid CIs) when naive estimation does not
151	
152	**Honest limitation**:
153	- EC-HTE's RMSE advantage over global correction is not demonstrated at the K=4 expert budgets tested
154	- The RMSE benefit of subgroup-specific correction requires either larger expert budgets or fewer subgroups (K=2)
155
```

```text
22	| gpt-3.5-turbo | FPR | 0.400 | 0.473 | 0.374 | 0.468 | 0.099 |

24	
25	### Key Finding 1: Cross-subgroup misclassification heterogeneity confirmed
26	
27	All 3 models show **Δπ > 0.05** across subgroups:
28	- gpt-4o: Δ_FPR=0.184, Δ_FNR=0.168 (overall Δπ=0.160)
29	- gpt-4o-mini: Δ_FPR=0.191, Δ_FNR=0.108 (overall Δπ=0.140)
30	- gpt-3.5-turbo: Δ_FPR=0.099, Δ_FNR=0.101 (overall Δπ=0.113)
31	
32	This validates the paper's core premise: LLM misclassification is structurally heterogeneous across text-feature-defined subgroups, and this holds on a second NLP dataset beyond TweetEval.
33	
34	### Key Finding 2: Severe FPR/FNR asymmetry
35	
36	gpt-4o-mini and gpt-3.5-turbo show highly asymmetric confusion matrices:
37	- gpt-4o-mini: FPR ≈ 0.42–0.61 while FNR ≈ 0.05–0.16 (over-predicts toxicity)
38	- gpt-3.5-turbo: FPR ≈ 0.37–0.47 while FNR ≈ 0.14–0.24
39	
40	This asymmetry causes mixing matrix ill-conditioning (rows become near-uniform when FPR ≈ 0.5), which amplifies estimation noise under matrix inversion.
41	
42	## 3. Semi-Synthetic DGP Results
43	
44	### 3a. Asymmetric CMs (real LLM confusion matrices)
45	
46	**DGP**: N=5000, τ(z=0)=0.10, τ(z=1)=0.40, MC=50, uniform CATE (no per-subgroup variation)
47	
48	Results at n_expert=250:
49

51	|-------|--------|-------|------|----------|
52	| gpt-3.5-turbo | naive | 0.1194 | 0.1374 | 0.708 |
53	| gpt-3.5-turbo | global_corrected | 0.1578 | 0.1963 | 0.948 |
54	| gpt-3.5-turbo | hb_ec_hte | 0.3188 | 0.4811 | 0.938 |
55	| gpt-4o | naive | 0.1015 | 0.1222 | 0.820 |
56	| gpt-4o | global_corrected | 0.1425 | 0.1745 | 0.945 |
57	| gpt-4o | hb_ec_hte | 0.2473 | 0.3629 | 0.912 |
58	| gpt-4o-mini | naive | 0.1303 | 0.1475 | 0.635 |
59	| gpt-4o-mini | global_corrected | 0.1581 | 0.1986 | 0.948 |
60	| gpt-4o-mini | hb_ec_hte | 0.2842 | 0.4201 | 0.938 |
61	
62	**Result**: EC-HTE has 2–3× worse RMSE than global correction with asymmetric CMs. Consistent with exp-015/016 failure analysis — asymmetric CMs with FPR ≈ 0.5 cause ill-conditioning.

136	| 500 | global_corrected | 0.0996 | 0.1220 | 0.950 |
137	| 500 | hb_ec_hte | 0.1262 | 0.1582 | 0.940 |
138	
139	## 4. Key Findings
140	
141	### Finding 1: Δπ > 0.05 confirmed across all models ✓
142	Civil Comments reproduces the subgroup-heterogeneous misclassification structure found in TweetEval. This holds for GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, and GPT-3.5-turbo across text-feature subgroups.
143	
144	### Finding 2: RMSE penalty is inherent to correction, not CM estimation
145	The v2 experiment includes **oracle_corrected** (uses the TRUE confusion matrix). Even this perfect-knowledge ceiling has worse RMSE than naive:
146	- Moderate: oracle_corrected RMSE=0.1229 vs naive=0.0992 (+24%)
147	- Extreme: oracle_corrected RMSE=0.1314 vs naive=0.1035 (+27%)
148	- real_gpt-3.5: oracle_corrected RMSE=0.2103 vs naive=0.1374 (+53%)
149	- real_gpt-4o-mini: oracle_corrected RMSE=0.2262 vs naive=0.1491 (+52%)
150	
151	This means the RMSE cost comes from **matrix inversion amplifying observation noise**, not from CM estimation error. No amount of partial pooling or larger expert budgets can close this gap — it is structural.
152	
153	### Finding 3: Coverage is EC-HTE's value proposition
154	Naive estimation has severely degraded coverage when misclassification is moderate-to-high:
155	- gpt-4o-mini rates: Naive coverage = 0.630–0.720 (target: 0.95)
156	- gpt-3.5-turbo rates: Naive coverage = 0.705–0.768
157	- gpt-4o rates: Naive coverage = 0.823–0.843
158	
159	All correction methods restore coverage to ~0.90–0.97. This is the genuine value of correction.
160	
161	### Finding 4: RMSE ordering is Naive < Global < EC-HTE across all settings
162	This is consistent across **both datasets** (TweetEval exp-003 and Civil Comments):
163	- No correction method achieves lower RMSE than naive at any sample size tested
164	- Global correction has lower RMSE than EC-HTE (avoids per-subgroup CM estimation variance)
165	- EC-HTE ≈ Stratified MLE (partial pooling has marginal effect at K=4)
166	
167	### Finding 5: EC-HTE vs Stratified MLE — partial pooling helps marginally
168	EC-HTE/StratMLE RMSE ratio ranges from 0.83 to 1.15 across settings. Partial pooling provides modest improvement at K=4 — consistent with the theoretical expectation that information sharing is limited with few subgroups.
169	
170	## 5. Interpretation
171	
172	The results are consistent across both datasets (TweetEval and Civil Comments):
173	
174	1. **Misclassification heterogeneity is a real structural property** of LLM annotations — not an artifact of one dataset.
175	
176	2. **The RMSE penalty of correction is structural**: Even with perfect CMs, matrix inversion amplifies observation noise more than it reduces bias. This is inherent to the mixing-matrix correction mechanism, not a limitation of any specific estimator.
177	
178	3. **Correction methods win on coverage/calibration**: When the researcher needs valid confidence intervals (not just point estimates), correction is essential. Naive CIs have severe under-coverage (0.63–0.84 vs target 0.95).
179	
180	4. **Asymmetric CMs (FPR ≠ FNR) worsen the problem**: Real LLM CMs are highly asymmetric (gpt-4o-mini: FPR=0.42–0.61, FNR=0.05–0.16), causing additional ill-conditioning beyond the symmetric case.
181	
182	## 6. What This Means for the Paper
183	
184	For Reviewer C1's concern about generalization beyond TweetEval:
185	
186	**Supported claims**:
187	- Subgroup-heterogeneous misclassification is a structural property of LLM annotations (confirmed on 2nd dataset with 3 commercial LLMs)
188	- Correction methods (including EC-HTE) restore calibrated inference when naive estimation severely under-covers
189	- EC-HTE provides modest improvement over stratified MLE via partial pooling
190	
191	**Honest limitation (consistent across both datasets)**:
192	- EC-HTE's RMSE is higher than both naive and global correction at all tested sample sizes
193	- This is inherent to matrix inversion correction (even oracle-corrected shows the same pattern)
194	- The RMSE benefit of subgroup-specific correction requires either much larger expert budgets or fewer subgroups (K=2)
195	- The primary value proposition is coverage calibration, not point estimation accuracy
196	
```
